\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(&s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big|\\
&=\big|q_{\varphi_{s}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j))}(s)-q_{\varphi_{s}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k))}(s)\big|\\
&\geq|\alpha|\cdot\big|\varphi_{s}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j))-\varphi_{s}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k))\big|-\big|\varphi_{s}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j))-\varphi_{s}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k))\big|^{1/2}\cdot(\log N)^{2}>3\mathfrak{M},\end{aligned}

where in the first statement we used (
1.15
); in the second we used (
3.3
) (and our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{2}
, with (
7.8
) to verify its assumption); and in the third we used the bound
|\varphi_{s}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j))-\varphi_{s}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k))|\geq\mathfrak{M}(\log N)^{5}
. To establish the second bound in (
7.9
), observe that

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(s)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(s)|\geq\big|Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big|-|w_{j}(s)|-|w_{k}(s)|\geq 3\mathfrak{M}-2B\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{20}>2\mathfrak{M},

where in the first statement we used the definition (
7.3
) of
w_{j}
; in the second we used the first inequality (
7.9
) with the second inequality in (
7.4
); and in the third we used (
6.4
).
∎

Proof of
Proposition

7.6
.

By
Lemma

7.4
(with our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{4}
), for each
i^{\prime}\in\llbracket 0,\mathfrak{T}\rrbracket
and
s\in[i^{\prime},\mathfrak{T}_{i^{\prime}}]
, the matrix
\bm{S}_{\bm{\Lambda};\bm{\mathfrak{Q}}(s)}^{\llbracket\ell_{i^{\prime}},m_{i^{\prime}}\rrbracket}
is invertible. Therefore, by
Definition

7.1
, (
7.2
) holds for the
i
there equal to any
i^{\prime}\in\llbracket 0,\mathfrak{T}\rrbracket
, and with the
(j,s)
there equal to any element of
\llbracket\ell_{i^{\prime}},m_{i^{\prime}}\rrbracket\times[i^{\prime},\mathfrak{T}_{i^{\prime}}]
. Thus, for such
(j,s)
, we have

(7.11)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Lambda_{j}&=\mathfrak{Q}_{j}^{\prime}(s)\cdot(1+T^{3}\cdot(\mathbbm{1}_{j\leq\ell_{i^{\prime}}+T^{2}}+\mathbbm{1}_{j\geq m_{i^{\prime}}-T^{2}}))\\
&\qquad+2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{i^{\prime}}}^{m_{i^{\prime}}}(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}^{\prime}(s)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}^{\prime}(s))\cdot\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(s)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(s)).\end{aligned}

Now set
i_{0}=i-1
, and denote
\ell_{i}^{\prime}=\ell_{i}-\lfloor T^{3}/2\rfloor
and
m_{i}^{\prime}=m_{i}+\lfloor T^{3}/2\rfloor
; in this way, we have
\llbracket\ell_{i},m_{i}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket\ell_{i}^{\prime},m_{i}^{\prime}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket\ell_{i_{0}},m_{i_{0}}\rrbracket
. Let us assume that
i^{\prime}\in\llbracket 0,i_{0}\rrbracket
and that
(j,s)\in\llbracket\ell_{i}^{\prime},m_{i}^{\prime}\rrbracket\times[i^{\prime},\mathfrak{T}_{i^{\prime}}]
. Since
j\in\llbracket\ell_{i}^{\prime},m_{i}^{\prime}\rrbracket
, and
\ell_{i}^{\prime}\geq\ell_{i^{\prime}}+T^{3}/2
and
m_{i}^{\prime}\leq m_{i^{\prime}}+T^{3}/2
, we have
\mathbbm{1}_{j\leq\ell_{i^{\prime}}+T^{2}}+\mathbbm{1}_{j\geq m_{i^{\prime}}-T^{2}}=0
. Also, since for any
k\notin\llbracket\ell_{i_{0}},m_{i_{0}}\rrbracket
we have by
Lemma

3.21
(with our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{3}
) and (
6.4
) that

\displaystyle\big|\varphi_{s}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j))-\varphi_{s}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k))\big|\geq|k-j|-4T(\log N)^{2}>T^{2}-4T(\log N)^{2}>\mathfrak{M}(\log N)^{5},

it follows from
Lemma

7.7
, and the inclusion
\supp\chi\subseteq[-\mathfrak{M},\mathfrak{M}]
(by
Assumption

6.1
), that
\chi^{\prime}(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(s)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(s))=0
whenever
k\notin\llbracket\ell_{i_{0}},m_{i_{0}}\rrbracket
. Inserting these into (
7.11
) yields for any
(j,s)\in\llbracket\ell_{i}^{\prime},m_{i}^{\prime}\rrbracket\times[0,\mathfrak{T}_{i_{0}}]
that

(7.12)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{j}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{Q}_{j}^{\prime}(s)+2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{i_{0}}}^{m_{i_{0}}}(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}^{\prime}(s)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}^{\prime}(s))\cdot\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(s)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(s)).

Integrating (
7.12
) over
s\in[0,t]
for any fixed
t\in[0,\mathfrak{T}_{i_{0}}]
yields for any
j\in\llbracket\ell_{i}^{\prime},m_{i}^{\prime}\rrbracket
that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{j}t

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(t)-\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(0)+2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{i_{0}}}^{m_{i_{0}}}\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\big(\chi(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(t)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(t))-\chi(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(0)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(0))\big).

Subtracting this from (
6.2
) (with the
k
there equal to
\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)
here); using
Lemma

7.7
with the facts that
\supp\chi\subseteq[-\mathfrak{M},\mathfrak{M}]
and that
(\ell_{i_{0}},m_{i_{0}})=(\ell_{i}^{\prime}-\lceil T^{3}/2\rceil,m_{i}^{\prime}+\lceil T^{3}/2\rceil)
to restrict the sum there to over
i=\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)
with
k\in\llbracket\ell_{i_{0}},m_{i_{0}}\rrbracket
(after also using (
3.4
), with our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{2}
, to restrict to
k\in\llbracket k_{1},k_{2}\rrbracket
); and using the fact that
\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(0)=Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(0)
then yields

\displaystyle\Bigg|w_{j}(t

\displaystyle)\cdot(1+T^{3}\cdot(\mathbbm{1}_{j\leq\ell_{i_{0}}+T^{2}}+\mathbbm{1}_{j\geq m_{i_{0}}-T^{2}}))

\displaystyle+2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{i_{0}}}^{m_{i_{0}}}\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\big(\chi(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(t)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(t))-\chi\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(t)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(t)\big)\big)\Bigg|\leq B\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{16},

where we also used the fact that
\mathbbm{1}_{j\leq\ell_{i_{0}}+T^{2}}+\mathbbm{1}_{j\geq m_{i_{0}}-T^{2}}=0
, since
j\in\llbracket\ell_{i}^{\prime},m_{i}^{\prime}\rrbracket
. Taylor expanding
\chi^{\prime}
, it follows that

(7.13)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Bigg|w_{j}&(t)\cdot(1+T^{3}\cdot(\mathbbm{1}_{j\leq\ell_{i_{0}}+T^{2}}+\mathbbm{1}_{j\geq m_{i_{0}}-T^{2}}))\\
&+2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{i_{0}}}^{m_{i_{0}}}\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot(w_{j}(t)-w_{k}(t))\cdot\chi^{\prime}(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(t)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(t))\Bigg|\\
&\leq 10B\mathfrak{M}^{-2}(\log N)^{2}\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{i_{0}}}^{m_{i_{0}}}w_{k}(t)^{2}\cdot\big(\mathbbm{1}_{|\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(t)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(t)|\leq\mathfrak{M}}+\mathbbm{1}_{|Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(t)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(t)|\leq\mathfrak{M}}\big)\\
&\qquad+B\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{16}\\
&\leq B\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{16}+10B\mathfrak{M}^{-2}(\log N)^{2}\cdot(B\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{20})^{2}\cdot 6\mathfrak{M}(\log N)^{5}\leq B\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{18}.\end{aligned}

Here, in the first bound we used
Assumption

6.1
and the fact that
\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\leq 5(\log N)^{2}
(by our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{1}
); in the second we used (
7.4
) and the fact that there are at most
6\mathfrak{M}(\log N)^{5}
indices
k\in\llbracket\ell_{i_{0}},m_{i_{0}}\rrbracket
for which the summand in (
7.13
) is nonzero (by
Lemma

7.7
and the fact that
\supp\chi^{\prime}\subseteq[-\mathfrak{M},\mathfrak{M}]
); and in the third we used (
6.4
).

The bound (
7.13
) applies if
j\in\llbracket\ell_{i}^{\prime},m_{i}^{\prime}\rrbracket
. If
j\in\llbracket\ell_{i_{0}},m_{i_{0}}\rrbracket\setminus\llbracket\ell_{i}^{\prime},m_{i}^{\prime}\rrbracket
, then we have

(7.14)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Bigg|w_{j}&(t)\cdot(1+T^{3}\cdot(\mathbbm{1}_{j\leq\ell_{i_{0}}+T^{2}}+\mathbbm{1}_{j\geq m_{i_{0}}-T^{2}}))\\
&+2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{i_{0}}}^{m_{i_{0}}}\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot(w_{j}(t)-w_{k}(t))\cdot\chi^{\prime}(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(t)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(t))\Bigg|\\
&\qquad\leq\big(T^{3}+1+4\cdot 5(\log N)^{5}\cdot B\mathfrak{M}^{-1}\cdot 3\mathfrak{M}(\log N)^{5}\big)\cdot\displaystyle\max_{j\in\llbracket\ell_{i_{0}},m_{i_{0}}\rrbracket}|w_{j}(t)|\leq T^{4}.\end{aligned}

Here, in the first bound we used the fact that
|\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})|\leq 5(\log N)^{2}
(by our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{1}
), that
|\chi^{\prime}(q)|\leq B\mathfrak{M}^{-1}
; and that there are at most
3\mathfrak{M}(\log N)^{5}
indices
k
for which
\chi^{\prime}(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(t)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(t))\neq 0
by
Lemma

7.7
and the fact that
\supp\chi^{\prime}\subseteq[-\mathfrak{M},\mathfrak{M}]
. In the second, we used (
7.4
) with (
6.4
).

Denoting
\bm{w}=(w_{\ell_{i_{0}}}(t),w_{\ell_{i_{0}}+1}(t),\ldots,w_{m_{i_{0}}}(t))\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{i_{0}}-\ell_{i_{0}}+1}
, we find from (
7.13
), (
7.14
), and (
6.6
) that

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{\bm{\Lambda};\bm{\mathfrak{Q}}(t)}^{\llbracket\ell_{i_{0}},m_{i_{0}}\rrbracket}\bm{w}=\bm{u},\qquad\text{where}\qquad|u_{k}|\leq B\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{18}+T^{4}\cdot(\mathbbm{1}_{j\leq\ell_{i}-\lfloor T^{3}/2\rfloor}+\mathbbm{1}_{j\geq m_{i}+\lfloor T^{3}/2\rfloor}),

and we have denoted
\bm{u}=(u_{\ell_{i_{0}}},u_{\ell_{i_{0}}+1},\ldots,u_{m_{i_{0}}})
. Recalling from (
7.5
) that
\bm{S}_{\bm{\Lambda};\bm{\mathfrak{Q}}(t)}^{\llbracket\ell_{i},m_{i}\rrbracket}
satisfies (
6.7
) with the
\varepsilon
there equal to
(2\log N)^{-1}
here, it follows from (
6.8
) (with the
U
there equal to
\mathfrak{M}^{-1}T^{2}\geq T
here) that

(7.15)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket\ell_{i},m_{i}\rrbracket}|w_{k}(t)|&\leq 2\log N\cdot\displaystyle\max_{k:|\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(t)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(t)|\leq\lfloor T^{3}/4\rfloor}|u_{k}|+2\log N\cdot e^{-T/16\log N}\cdot T^{4}\\
&\leq 2B\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{19}+N^{-1}\leq 3B\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{19},\end{aligned}

where we also used the fact that
|j-k|\leq\lfloor T^{3}/4\rfloor
whenever
|\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(t)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(t)|\leq T^{2}
(by reasoning entirely analogous to that used in the proof of
Lemma

7.7
). This confirms (
7.6
) for all
t\in[0,\mathfrak{T}_{i_{0}}]
and thus (as
i_{0}=i-1
) for all
t\in[0,\mathfrak{T}_{i}-1]
, thereby establishing the proposition.
∎

8.
Analysis of the Proxy Dynamics

In this section we prove
Theorem

1.13
, by analyzing the proxy dynamics
(\mathfrak{Q}_{j})
from
Definition

7.1
. We begin in
Section

8.1
by showing as
Lemma

8.1
that the effective velocities
v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})
approximately satisfy the relation (
7.2
) defining the
(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}^{\prime})
. We then use this to approximate the derivatives of
\mathfrak{Q}_{j}
by the
v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})
in
Section

8.2
, through
Proposition

8.3
. This quickly yields
Theorem

1.13
under more restrictive hypotheses (
Corollary

8.4
), which we remove in
Section

8.3
.

8.1.
Approximate Relation for Effective Velocities

In this section we establish the following lemma, which indicates that setting
\mathfrak{Q}_{j}^{\prime}(s)\approx v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})
approximately yields a solution of (
7.2
) (though with the
\mathfrak{Q}_{j}
there replaced by
Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}
here). Throughout this section, we adopt
Assumption

6.4
, recall
Definition

7.1
, let
\theta_{0}=\theta_{0}(\beta)>0
denote the constant from
Lemma

3.9
, and assume that
\theta\in(0,\theta_{0})
.

Lemma 8.1
.

The following holds with overwhelming probability. Let
s\in[0,T]
be a real number, and let
j,\ell,m\in\llbracket N_{1},N_{2}\rrbracket
be indices satisfying

(8.1)

\displaystyle j,\ell,m\in\llbracket N_{1}+T(\log N)^{6},N_{2}-T(\log N)^{6}\rrbracket;\qquad j\in\llbracket\ell+T(\log N)^{5},m-T(\log N)^{5}\rrbracket.

Then, we have that

(8.2)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Bigg|&\Lambda_{j}-v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})\cdot\bigg(2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell}^{m}\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)+1\bigg)\\
&\qquad\qquad+2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell}^{m}v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{k})\cdot\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)\Bigg|\leq B\mathfrak{M}^{-1/2}(\log N)^{21}.\end{aligned}

We first show the below variant of
Lemma

8.1
addressing a fixed time
s\in[0,T]
.

Lemma 8.2
.

There exists a constant
c>0
such that the below holds. Fix a real number
s\in[0,T]
. The following holds with probability at least
1-c^{-1}e^{-c(\log N)^{2}}
. For any indices
j,\ell,m\in\llbracket N_{1},N_{2}\rrbracket
satisfying (
8.1
), we have

(8.3)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Bigg|&\Lambda_{j}-v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})\cdot\bigg(2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell}^{m}\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)+1\bigg)\\
&\qquad\quad+2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell}^{m}v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{k})\cdot\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)\Bigg|\leq\displaystyle\frac{B}{2}\cdot\mathfrak{M}^{-1/2}(\log N)^{21}.\end{aligned}

Proof.

Throughout this proof, recalling
Definition

3.12
, we restrict to the event
\mathsf{E}_{1}=\mathsf{BND}_{\bm{L}(0)}(\log N)
, as we may by
Lemma

3.13
. We further restrict to the event
\mathsf{E}_{2}
on which
Lemma

3.11
holds, and to the event
\mathsf{E}_{3}
on which
Lemma

3.21
holds.

We will further restrict to a fourth event
\mathsf{E}_{4}
, which will be obtained by applying
Proposition

4.4
with the
(f,G)
there equal to
(\mathfrak{l},\chi^{\prime})
here, and the
F
there equal to either
v_{\eff}
or
1
here. Then, we may take the parameters
(S,B)
in
Assumption

4.1
to be
(\mathfrak{M},B)
here (by
Assumption

6.1
), and the parameter
A
there to be
(\log N)^{2}
here (by
Corollary

3.8
). Moreover, we may take the parameter
D
in (
4.3
) to be
(\log N)^{3}
, by the definition (
6.1
) of
\mathfrak{l}
. Therefore, since
Assumption

6.1
implies that
\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\chi^{\prime}(\alpha q)dq=\alpha^{-1}
,
Proposition

4.4
yields an overwhelmingly probable event
\mathsf{E}_{4}
, on which the following holds. Whenever
k\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket
satisfies
N_{1}+T(\log N)^{5}\leq\varphi_{s}(k)\leq N_{2}-T(\log N)^{5}
, we have

(8.4)

\displaystyle\Bigg|2\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathfrak{l}(\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{i})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{k}(s)-Q_{i}(s)\big)-2\alpha^{-1}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathfrak{l}(\lambda_{k}-\lambda)\varrho(\lambda)d\lambda\Bigg|\leq B\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{18},

and

(8.5)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Bigg|2\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}v_{\eff}(\lambda_{i})\cdot\mathfrak{l}(\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{i})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{k}(s)-Q_{i}(s)\big)-2\alpha^{-1}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}v_{\eff}(\lambda)\mathfrak{l}(&\lambda_{k}-\lambda)\varrho(\lambda)d\lambda\Bigg|\\
&\leq B\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{18}.\end{aligned}

We additionally further restrict
\mathsf{E}_{4}
in the below.

Let us first approximate the integrals appearing in (
8.4
) and (
8.5
). To that end, observe that there exist constants
c_{2}>0
and
C_{1}>1
such that, for sufficiently large
N
,

(8.6)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Bigg|\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathfrak{l}(x-y)v_{\eff}(y&)\varrho(y)dy-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\log|x-y|v_{\eff}(y)\varrho(y)dy\Bigg|\\
&\leq C_{1}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\big|\mathfrak{l}(x-y)-\log|x-y|\big|\cdot(|y|+1)e^{-c_{2}y^{2}}dy\leq e^{-(\log N)^{2}}.\end{aligned}

Here, the first inequality follows from the fact that
|v_{\eff}(x)|\leq C_{2}(|x|+1)
for some
C_{2}>1
(by
Corollary

3.8
), with the exponential decay of
\varrho
(from
Lemma

3.1
), and the second follows from the fact that

\displaystyle\big|\mathfrak{l}(x)-\log|x|\big|\leq 2|\log x|\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{|x|\leq e^{-2(\log N)^{2}}}+e^{-2(\log N)^{2}},

which is a quick consequence of the definition (
6.1
) of
\mathfrak{l}
. By entirely analogous reasoning, we also find for sufficiently large
N
that

(8.7)

\displaystyle\Bigg|\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathfrak{l}(x-y)\varrho(y)dy-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\log|x-y|\varrho(y)dy\Bigg|\leq e^{-(\log N)^{2}}.

We next insert (
8.6
) and (
8.7
) into (
8.4
) and (
8.5
), respectively, and additionally change variables from
(i,k)
to
(\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k),\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j))
. This yields

(8.8)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Bigg|2\displaystyle\sum_{k=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)-2\alpha^{-1}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}&\log|\Lambda_{j}-\lambda|\varrho(\lambda)d\lambda\Bigg|\\
&\leq 2B\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{18},\end{aligned}

and

(8.9)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Bigg|2\displaystyle\sum_{k=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{k})\cdot\mathfrak{l}(&\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)\\
&\qquad-2\alpha^{-1}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\log|\Lambda_{j}-\lambda|v_{\eff}(\lambda)\varrho(\lambda)d\lambda\Bigg|\leq 2B\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{18},\end{aligned}

where we used the fact that
N_{1}+T(\log N)^{5}\leq\varphi_{s}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j))\leq N_{2}-T(\log N)^{5}
(by (
8.1
), since
Lemma

3.21
with our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{3}
implies that have
|\varphi_{s}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j))-j|\leq 2T(\log N)^{2}
). Combining these bounds, and using the fact from
Corollary

3.8
(with our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{1}
) that
|v_{\eff}(\lambda)|\leq(\log N)^{2}
for sufficiently large
N
and any
\lambda\in\eig\bm{L}
, yields

(8.10)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Bigg|&\Lambda_{j}-v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})\cdot\bigg(2\displaystyle\sum_{k=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)+1\bigg)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2\displaystyle\sum_{k=N_{1}}^{N_{2}}v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{k})\cdot\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)\Bigg|\\
&\leq\Bigg|\Lambda_{j}-v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})\cdot\bigg(2\alpha^{-1}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\log|\Lambda_{j}-\lambda|\varrho(\lambda)d\lambda+1\bigg)+2\alpha^{-1}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\log|\Lambda_{j}-\lambda|v_{\eff}(\lambda)\varrho(\lambda)d\lambda\Bigg|\\
&\qquad+B\mathfrak{M}^{-1/2}(\log N)^{21}=B\mathfrak{M}^{-1/2}(\log N)^{21}.\end{aligned}

Here, in the last equality we used the fact that

\displaystyle v_{\eff}

\displaystyle(\Lambda_{j})\cdot\bigg(2\alpha^{-1}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\log|\Lambda_{j}-\lambda|\varrho(\lambda)d\lambda+1\bigg)-2\alpha^{-1}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\log|\Lambda_{j}-\lambda|v_{\eff}(\lambda)\varrho(\lambda)d\lambda

\displaystyle=v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})\cdot\big(\alpha^{-1}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{T}}\varrho(\Lambda_{j})+1\big)-\alpha^{-1}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{T}\varrho}v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})=(\theta^{-1}\cdot\bm{\varsigma_{0}^{\dr}}-\alpha^{-1}\cdot\bm{\mathrm{T}\varrho})v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})=\Lambda_{j},

where the first statement holds by the definition (
1.13
) of
\bm{\mathrm{T}}
; the second holds by the second statement in (
3.2
); and the third holds by
Lemma

3.5
.

It therefore remains to show that the sums over
k
on the left side of (
8.10
) can be restricted to the interval
\llbracket\ell,m\rrbracket
. To that end, it suffices to verify that
\chi^{\prime}(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s))=0
if
j\in\llbracket\ell+T(\log N)^{5},m-T(\log N)^{5}\rrbracket
and
k\notin\llbracket\ell,m\rrbracket
, and hence (as
\supp\chi^{\prime}\subseteq[-\mathfrak{M},\mathfrak{M}]
) to confirm for
|j-k|\geq T(\log N)^{5}
that
|Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)|>\mathfrak{M}
. This follows very similarly to as in the proof of (
7.10
), as a quick consequence of
Lemma

3.11
and
Lemma

3.21
(with our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{2}\cap\mathsf{E}_{3}
); further details are therefore omitted.
∎

Now we can establish
Lemma

8.1
.

Proof of
Lemma

8.1
.

Throughout this proof, recalling
Definition

3.12
, we restrict to the event
\mathsf{E}_{1}=\mathsf{BND}_{\bm{L}}(\log N)
, which we may by
Lemma

3.13
. We further restrict to the event
\mathsf{E}_{2}
on which
Lemma

3.21
holds and to the event
\mathsf{E}_{3}
on which
Lemma

3.22
holds. Additionally, we restrict to the event
\mathsf{E}_{4}
on which (
7.9
) holds for any
k\in\llbracket\ell,m\rrbracket
, as we may by
Lemma

7.7
.
11
11

11

While
Lemma

7.7
was claimed for indices
j,k\in\llbracket k_{1},k_{2}\rrbracket
, it is quickly verified from the deriviation in (
7.10
) that the first bound in (
7.9
) holds for
j
satisfying (
8.1
) and
k\in\llbracket\ell,m\rrbracket
.
For any
r\in[0,T]
, also let
\mathsf{E}_{5}(r)
denote the event on which (
8.3
) holds. Let
\mathcal{T}\subset[0,T]
be an
N^{-1}
-mesh of
[0,T]
, and set
\mathsf{E}_{5}=\bigcap_{r\in\mathcal{T}}\mathsf{E}_{5}(r)
; we may also restrict to
\mathsf{E}_{5}
by
Lemma

8.2
, with a union bound.

Now fix
s\in[0,T]
, and let
s_{0}\in\mathcal{S}
be such that
|s-s_{0}|\leq N^{-1}
. Since (by our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{5}
) (
8.3
) holds with the
s
there equal to
s_{0}
here, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket\ell,m\rrbracket}|v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{k})|\cdot\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket\ell,m\rrbracket}|\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})|

\displaystyle\qquad\times\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell}^{m}\big|\chi^{\prime}(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)-\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s_{0})-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s_{0})\big)\big|\leq B\mathfrak{M}^{-1/2}(\log N)^{20}.

Due to our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{1}
, we have for any
k\in\llbracket N_{1},N_{2}\rrbracket
that
|\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})|\leq 5(\log N)^{2}
and
|v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{k})|\leq(\log N)^{2}
(the latter by
Corollary

3.8
). Hence, it remains to show that

(8.11)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell}^{m}\big|\chi^{\prime}(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)-\chi^{\prime}\big(

\displaystyle Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s_{0})-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s_{0})\big)\big|\leq B\mathfrak{M}^{-1/2}(\log N)^{15}.

Next, by
Lemma

3.21
(with our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{2}
), we have for any
k\in\llbracket\ell,m\rrbracket
that
|\varphi_{s}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k))-k|\leq 2T(\log N)^{2}
, and so
\varphi_{s}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k))\in\llbracket\ell+2T(\log N)^{2},m-2T(\log N)^{2}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket N_{1}+T(\log N)^{5},N_{2}-T(\log N)^{5}\rrbracket
, by (
8.1
). Therefore, by
Lemma

3.22
(with our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{2}
) with the fact that
|s-s_{0}|\leq N^{-1}\leq 1/2
, we have for any
k\in\llbracket\ell,m\rrbracket
that

\displaystyle\big|\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)-\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s_{0})-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s_{0})\big)\big|\leq 3(\log N)^{4}.

Thus, by Taylor expanding
\chi^{\prime}
(and using
Assumption

6.1
), we deduce

(8.12)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell}^{m}\big|\chi^{\prime}\big(&Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)-\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s_{0})-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s_{0})\big)\big|\\
&\leq 3B\mathfrak{M}^{-2}(\log N)^{4}\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell}^{m}\big(\mathbbm{1}_{|Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)|\leq\mathfrak{M}}+\mathbbm{1}_{|Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s_{0})-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s_{0})|\leq\mathfrak{M}}\big).\end{aligned}

By the first inequality in (
7.9
) (with our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{4}
), there are at most
3\mathfrak{M}(\log N)^{5}
indices
k
for which the summand on the right side of (
8.12
) is nonzero, in which case it is at most equal to
2
. It follows that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell}^{m}\big|\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)-\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s_{0})-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s_{0})\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq 18B\mathfrak{M}^{-1}(\log N)^{9}

\displaystyle\leq B\mathfrak{M}^{-1/2}(\log N)^{15}

where in the last inequality we used (
6.4
). This confirms (
8.11
) and thus the lemma. ∎

8.2.
Derivative Approximation for
\mathfrak{Q}_{j}

In this section we use
Lemma

8.1
to prove the following proposition indicating that the derivative
\mathfrak{Q}_{j}^{\prime}
of the proxy dynamics is close to
v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})
, with high probability.

Proposition 8.3
.

Adopt
Assumption

6.4
, recall
Definition

7.1
, let
\theta_{0}=\theta_{0}(\beta)>0
denote the constant from
Lemma

3.9
, and assume that
\theta\in(0,\theta_{0})
. The following holds with overwhelming probability. For each
j\in\llbracket k_{1}+T^{5},k_{2}-T^{5}\rrbracket
and
s\in[0,T]
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{Q}_{j}^{\prime}(s)-v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})|\leq B^{2}\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{32}.

Proof of
Proposition

8.3
.

Throughout this proof, we assume for notational convenience that
T
is an integer. Recalling
Definition

3.12
, we restrict to the event
\mathsf{E}_{1}=\mathsf{BND}_{\bm{L}}(\log N)
throughout, as we may by
Lemma

3.13
. We further restrict to the events
\mathsf{E}_{2}
on which
Lemma

3.11
holds;
\mathsf{E}_{3}
on which
Lemma

3.21
holds;
\mathsf{E}_{4}
on which
Proposition

7.3
,
Lemma

7.4
, and
Proposition

7.5
all hold;
\mathsf{E}_{5}
on which
Lemma

7.7
holds; and
\mathsf{E}_{6}
on which
Lemma

8.1
holds.

First observe, since
\supp\chi^{\prime}\subseteq[-\mathfrak{M},\mathfrak{M}]
(by
Assumption

6.1
),
Lemma

7.7
(and our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{5}
) implies that
\chi^{\prime}(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(s)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(s))=0
if
|j-k|\geq T^{2}
. Therefore, this in particular holds if
j\in\llbracket\ell_{T}+T^{2},m_{T}-T^{2}\rrbracket
and
k\notin\llbracket\ell_{T},m_{T}\rrbracket
(where we recall the
(\ell_{i},m_{i})
from
Definition

7.1
). Moreover, by our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{4}
, (
7.2
) holds for any
i\in\llbracket 0,T\rrbracket
, for all
j\in\llbracket\ell_{T},m_{T}\rrbracket
and
s\in[i,i+1]
. Together, these two facts imply for any
j\in\llbracket\ell_{T}+T^{2},m_{T}-T^{2}\rrbracket
and
s\in[0,T]
that

(8.13)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Lambda_{j}&=\mathfrak{Q}_{j}^{\prime}(s)\cdot\Bigg(2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{T}}^{m_{T}}\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(s)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(s)\big)+1\Bigg)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{T}}^{m_{T}}\mathfrak{Q}_{k}^{\prime}(s)\cdot\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(s)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(s)\big).\end{aligned}

By
Lemma

8.1
(and our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{6}
),
v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})
satisfies a similar equation, namely, we have for any
j\in\llbracket\ell_{T},m_{T}\rrbracket
(where we observe that
\llbracket\ell_{T},m_{T}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket k_{1},k_{2}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket N_{1}+2T(\log N)^{6},N_{2}-2T(\log N)^{6}\rrbracket
by (
6.5
) and (
6.4
)) and
s\in[0,T]
that

(8.14)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Bigg|&\Lambda_{j}-v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})\cdot\Bigg(2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{T}}^{m_{T}}\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)+1\Bigg)\\
&\qquad\quad+2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{T}}^{m_{T}}v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{k})\cdot\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)\Bigg|\leq B\mathfrak{M}^{-1/2}(\log N)^{21}.\end{aligned}

A distinction between this bound and (
8.13
) is that the latter replaces
Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(i)}(s)
with
\mathfrak{Q}_{i}(s)
, so let us bound the difference between the associated terms. By the second property in
Proposition

7.3
(with our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{4}
), we have that
|\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)|\leq B\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{20}
for each
k\in\llbracket\ell_{T},m_{T}\rrbracket
and
s\in[0,T]
. So it follows for any such
k
, and
j\in\llbracket\ell_{T}+T^{2},m_{T}-T^{2}\rrbracket
, that

(8.15)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\chi^{\prime}\big(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(s)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(s&)\big)-\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)\big|\\
&\leq B^{2}\mathfrak{M}^{-3/2}(\log N)^{20}\cdot\big(\mathbbm{1}_{|\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(s)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(s)|\leq\mathfrak{M}}+\mathbbm{1}_{|Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)|\leq\mathfrak{M}}\big),\end{aligned}

where we have also used
Assumption

6.1
. By
Lemma

7.7
(and our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{5}
), there are at most
3\mathfrak{M}(\log N)^{5}
indices
k\in\llbracket\ell_{T},m_{T}\rrbracket
for which at least one indicator function on the right side of (
8.15
) is nonzero. Moreover, our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{1}
implies that
|v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{k})|\leq(\log N)^{2}
and
|\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})|\leq 5(\log N)^{2}
for each
k\in\llbracket\ell_{T},m_{T}\rrbracket
(using
Corollary

3.8
for the former). Inserting these bounds, with (
8.15
), into (
8.14
) yields

(8.16)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Bigg|&\Lambda_{j}-v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})\cdot\bigg(2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{T}}^{m_{T}}\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(s)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(s))+1\bigg)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{T}}^{m_{T}}v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{k})\cdot\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}(s)-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}(s))\Bigg|\\
&\qquad\quad\leq B\mathfrak{M}^{-1/2}(\log N)^{21}+2\cdot(\log N)^{2}\cdot 5(\log N)^{2}\cdot 2B^{2}\mathfrak{M}^{-3/2}(\log N)^{20}\cdot 3\mathfrak{M}(\log N)^{5}\\
&\qquad\quad\leq 61B^{2}\mathfrak{M}^{-1/2}(\log N)^{29}.\end{aligned}

Now, fix
s\in[0,T]
; denote
\mathfrak{w}_{j}(r)=\mathfrak{Q}_{j}^{\prime}(s)-v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})
for each
j\in\llbracket\ell_{T},m_{T}\rrbracket
; and set
\bm{\mathfrak{w}}=(\mathfrak{w}_{\ell_{T}},\mathfrak{w}_{\ell_{T}+1},\ldots,\mathfrak{w}_{m_{T}})
. Denoting
\bm{\mathfrak{Q}}=(\mathfrak{Q}_{N_{1}}(s),\mathfrak{Q}_{N_{1}+1}(s),\ldots,\mathfrak{Q}_{N_{2}}(s))
for each
s\in[0,T]
, abbreviate the matrix
\bm{S}=\bm{S}_{\bm{\Lambda};\bm{\mathfrak{Q}}}^{\llbracket\ell_{T},m_{T}\rrbracket}
(from
Assumption

6.4
). Let
\bm{S}\bm{\mathfrak{w}}=\bm{\mathfrak{u}}
, where
\bm{\mathfrak{u}}=(\mathfrak{u}_{\ell_{T}},\mathfrak{u}_{\ell_{T}+1},\ldots,\mathfrak{u}_{m_{T}})
. We claim for each
j\in\llbracket\ell_{T},m_{T}\rrbracket
that

(8.17)

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{u}_{j}|\leq B^{2}\mathfrak{M}^{-1/2}(\log N)^{30}+\mathbbm{1}_{j\notin\llbracket\ell_{T}+T^{2},m_{T}-T^{2}\rrbracket}\cdot N^{6}.

If
j\in\llbracket\ell_{T}+T^{2},m_{T}-T^{2}\rrbracket
, we deduce by subtracting (
8.16
) from (
8.13
) that

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{u}_{j}|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\mathfrak{w}_{j}\cdot\bigg(2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{T}}^{m_{T}}\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)+1\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{T}}^{m_{T}}\mathfrak{w}_{k}\cdot\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})\cdot\chi^{\prime}\big(Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j)}(s)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(k)}(s)\big)\Bigg|\leq B^{2}\mathfrak{M}^{-1/2}(\log N)^{30},

where we have recalled that
\bm{\mathfrak{u}}=\bm{S}\bm{\mathfrak{w}}
and the definition of
\bm{S}
from
Assumption

6.4
; this confirms (
8.17
) these
j
. For the remaining
j\in\llbracket\ell_{T},m_{T}\rrbracket
, we have for sufficiently large
N
that

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{u}_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathfrak{w}_{j}|\cdot(T^{3}+1)+2\displaystyle\sum_{k=\ell_{T}}^{m_{T}}(|\mathfrak{w}_{j}|+|\mathfrak{w}_{k}|)\cdot|\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})|\cdot|\chi^{\prime}(\mathfrak{Q}_{j}-Q_{k})|

\displaystyle\leq 4N^{4}\cdot\displaystyle\sup_{q\in\mathbb{R}}|\chi^{\prime}(q)|\cdot\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket\ell_{T},m_{T}\rrbracket}|\mathfrak{w}_{k}|\cdot\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket\ell_{T},m_{T}\rrbracket}|\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})|\leq N^{5}\cdot\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket\ell_{T},m_{T}\rrbracket}|\mathfrak{w}_{k}|\leq N^{6},

where in the first statement we recalled the definition (
6.6
) of
\bm{S}
; in the second we used the facts that
T\leq N
and
m_{T}-\ell_{T}+1\leq N
; in the third we used the second statement in
Assumption

6.1
, (
6.4
), and the fact that
|\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k})|\leq 5(\log N)^{2}
(by our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{1}
); and in the fourth we used the fact that
|\mathfrak{w}_{k}|\leq|\mathfrak{Q}_{j}^{\prime}(s)|+|v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})|\leq 4(\log N)^{3}\leq N
(as we have
|\mathfrak{Q}_{j}^{\prime}(s)|\leq(\log N)^{3}
, by
Proposition

7.3
and our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{4}
, and
|v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})|\leq(\log N)^{2}
, by
Corollary

3.8
and our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{1}
). This establishes (
8.17
) in general.

By
Lemma

7.4
(with our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{4}
), (
7.5
) holds, thereby enabling us to apply
Lemma

6.5
, with the
\varepsilon
there equal to
(2\log N)^{-1}
here. The
U=\mathfrak{M}^{-1}T^{2}
case of (
6.8
) then yields for
j\in\llbracket\ell_{T}+2T^{3},m_{T}-2T^{3}\rrbracket
that

(8.18)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big|\mathfrak{Q}_{j}^{\prime}(s)-v_{\eff}(\Lambda_{j})\big|=|\mathfrak{w}_{j}|&\leq 2\log N\cdot\displaystyle\max_{k:|\mathfrak{Q}_{j}-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}|\leq T^{2}}|\mathfrak{u}_{k}|+2\log N\cdot e^{-T/16\log N}\cdot N^{6}\\
&\leq 2\log N\cdot\displaystyle\max_{k:|j-k|\leq T^{3}}|\mathfrak{u}_{k}|+N^{-1}\leq B^{2}\mathfrak{M}^{-1/2}(\log N)^{32},\end{aligned}

where we also used (
8.17
) and the fact that
|\mathfrak{Q}_{j}-\mathfrak{Q}_{k}|\leq T^{3}
implies that
|j-k|\leq T^{2}
, as quickly follows from
Lemma

3.11
and
Lemma

3.21
, with our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{2}\cap\mathsf{E}_{3}
(entirely analogously to in the proof of
Lemma

7.7
). This, together with the fact that
\llbracket\ell_{T}+2T^{3},m_{T}-2T^{3}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket k_{1}+T^{5},k_{2}-T^{5}\rrbracket
(as
(\ell_{T},m_{T})=(k_{1}+T^{4},k_{2}-T^{4})
), implies the proposition.
∎

A quick consequence of
Proposition

8.3
is the following corollary; it indicates that
Theorem

1.13
holds under more restrictive hypotheses (
8.19
) than (
1.14
) and (
1.16
).

Corollary 8.4
.

Adopt
Assumption

1.12
; let
\theta_{0}=\theta_{0}(\beta)>0
denote the constant from
Lemma

3.9
; and suppose that
\theta\in(0,\theta_{0})
. The following holds with overwhelming probability. Let
j\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket
be an index, and assume that

(8.19)

\displaystyle 10^{8}\cdot(\log N)^{60}\leq T\leq N^{1/10},\quad\text{and}\quad N_{1}+2T^{5}\leq\varphi_{0}(j)\leq N_{2}-2T^{5},

we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|Q_{j}(t)-Q_{j}(0)-tv_{\eff}(\lambda_{j})\big|\leq T^{1/2}(\log N)^{33}.

Proof.

This will follow from
Proposition

8.3
and
Proposition

7.3
, where we recall the notation from
Definition

7.1
throughout this proof. We must first set the parameters
(B,\mathfrak{M},k_{1},k_{2})
implicit in the statements of those results. So, let us set
B=100
(which guarantees the existence of
\chi
as in
Assumption

6.1
);
\mathfrak{M}=T
; and
k_{1}=N_{1}+T^{2}
and
k_{2}=N_{2}-T^{2}
. Under this setup, observe that (
6.4
) and (
6.5
) hold (due to (
8.19
)); we therefore adopt
Assumption

6.4
and define the proxy dynamics
\bm{\mathfrak{Q}}(s)
as in
Definition

7.1
. For the remainder of this proof, we restrict to the event
\mathsf{E}
on which both
Proposition

7.3
and
Proposition

8.3
hold (for the above choices of parameters).

Then by
Proposition

8.3
, for any
j_{0}\in\llbracket N_{1}+2T^{5},N_{2}-2T^{5}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket k_{1}+T^{5},k_{2}-T^{5}\rrbracket
and
t\in[0,T]
, we have

(8.20)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\max_{t\in[0,T]}\big|\mathfrak{Q}_{j_{0}}(t)-\mathfrak{Q}_{j_{0}}(0)-tv_{\eff}(\lambda_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j_{0})})\big|\leq T\cdot B^{2}\mathfrak{M}^{-1/2}(\log N)^{32}=10^{4}\cdot T^{1/2}(\log N)^{32},

where we have recalled from
Definition

7.1
that
\Lambda_{j_{0}}=\lambda_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j_{0})}
. Moreover, by
Proposition

7.3
we have for
j_{0}\in\llbracket N_{1}+2T^{5},N_{2}-2T^{5}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket k_{1}+T^{4},k_{2}-T^{4}\rrbracket\subseteq\llbracket\ell_{\lfloor T\rfloor},m_{\lfloor T\rfloor}\rrbracket
that

(8.21)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\max_{t\in[0,T]}\big|\mathfrak{Q}_{j_{0}}(t)-Q_{\varphi_{0}^{-1}(j_{0})}(t)\big|\leq B\mathfrak{M}^{1/2}(\log N)^{20}=100T^{1/2}(\log N)^{20}.

Combining (
8.20
) and (
8.21
), and setting
j_{0}=\varphi_{0}(j)
, we deduce the corollary.
∎

8.3.
Proof of
Theorem

1.13

Proof of
Theorem

1.13
.

First observe that if
T\leq 10^{8}\cdot(30\log N)^{60}
then we have
T^{1/2}(\log N)^{35}\geq(T+1)(\log N)^{4}
, and so the theorem follows from the
(t,t^{\prime};\lambda;\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})=(0,t;\lambda_{j};\varphi_{0}(j),\varphi_{t}(j))
case of
Lemma

3.22
. Therefore, we assume that
T\geq 10^{8}\cdot(30\log N)^{60}
in what follows.

We will show for any fixed
t\in[0,T]
that, with overwhelming probability,

(8.22)

\displaystyle\big|Q_{j}(t)-Q_{j}(0)-tv_{\eff}(\lambda_{j})\big|\leq T^{1/2}(\log N)^{34}.

Let us first verify that this is sufficient to confirm the theorem. Recalling
Definition

3.12
, restrict to the event
\mathsf{F}_{1}=\mathsf{BND}_{\bm{L}}(\log N)
from
Definition

3.12
, which we may by
Lemma

3.13
. We also restrict to the event
\mathsf{F}_{2}
on which
Lemma

3.11
holds and to the event
\mathsf{F}_{3}
on which
Lemma

3.22
holds.

Let
\mathsf{F}_{4}(t)
denote the event on which (
8.22
) holds; let
\mathcal{T}\subseteq[0,T]
denote an
N^{-2}
-mesh of
[0,T]
; and let
\mathsf{F}_{4}=\bigcap_{s\in\mathcal{T}}\mathsf{F}_{4}(s)
. Restrict to
\mathsf{F}_{4}
, and fix
t\in[0,T]
. Then, there exists
s\in\mathcal{S}
for which
|t-s|\leq N^{-2}
, meaning that

\displaystyle\big|Q_{j}(t)-Q_{j}(0)-tv_{\eff}(\lambda_{j})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|Q_{j}(s)-Q_{j}(0)-sv_{\eff}(\lambda_{j})\big|+|t-s|\cdot|v_{\eff}(\lambda_{j})|+|Q_{j}(t)-Q_{j}(s)|

\displaystyle\leq T^{1/2}(\log N)^{34}+N^{-2}\cdot(\log N)^{2}+|Q_{j}(t)-Q_{j}(s)|

\displaystyle\leq T^{1/2}(\log N)^{34}+N^{-1}+2(\log N)^{4}\leq T^{1/2}(\log N)^{35}.

Here, the second inequality follows from our restriction to
\mathsf{F}_{4}
and the fact that
|v_{\eff}(\lambda_{j})|\leq(\log N)^{2}
(by
Corollary

3.8
and our restriction to
\mathsf{F}_{1}
); the third from
Lemma

3.22
(with our restriction to
\mathsf{F}_{3}
), as
|t-s|\leq 1
; and the fourth from the fact that
N
is sufficiently large. This establishes the lemma, so it suffices to show that (
8.22
) holds with overwhelming probability.

To that end we first apply
Corollary

8.4
on a Toda lattice (at thermal equilibrium) on a larger interval, and then use comparison estimates (such as
Lemma

3.16
,
Lemma

3.19
, and
Lemma

3.20
) to approximate the original Toda lattice by the enlarged one. This will proceed similarly to in the proof of
[
1
, Theorem 8.5]
given
[
1
, Theorem 8.2]
. To implement it, first let
\tilde{N}_{1}\leq\tilde{N}_{2}
be integers satisfying

(8.23)

\displaystyle\tilde{N}_{1}+N^{10}\leq N_{1}\leq N_{2}\leq\tilde{N}_{2}-N^{10};\qquad T^{15}\leq\tilde{N}\leq N^{30},

where
\tilde{N}=\tilde{N}_{2}-\tilde{N}_{1}+1
. Let
(\tilde{\bm{a}}(s);\tilde{\bm{b}}(s))\in\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{N}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{N}}
denote the Flaschka variables for a Toda lattice on
\llbracket\tilde{N}_{1},\tilde{N}_{2}\rrbracket
; letting
\tilde{\bm{a}}(s)=(\tilde{a}_{\tilde{N}_{1}}(s),\tilde{a}_{\tilde{N}_{1}+1}(s),\ldots,\tilde{a}_{\tilde{N}_{2}}(s))
and
\tilde{\bm{b}}(s)=(\tilde{b}_{\tilde{N}_{1}}(s),\tilde{b}_{\tilde{N}_{1}+1}(s),\ldots,\tilde{b}_{\tilde{N}_{2}}(s))
, they satisfy
\tilde{a}_{\tilde{N}_{2}}(s)=0
, and (
1.8
) holds for each
(j,t)\in\llbracket\tilde{N}_{1},\tilde{N}_{2}\rrbracket\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. We sample the initial data
(\tilde{\bm{a}}(0);\tilde{\bm{b}}(0))
according to the thermal equilibrium
\mu_{\beta,\theta;\tilde{N}-1,\tilde{N}}
of
Definition

1.1
; we couple
(\tilde{\bm{a}}(0);\tilde{\bm{b}}(0))
with
(\bm{a}(0);\bm{b}(0))
so that
(\tilde{a}_{i}(0),\tilde{b}_{i}(0))=(a_{i}(0),b_{i}(0))
for all
i\in\llbracket N_{1},N_{2}-1\rrbracket
.

For any
s\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, denote the Lax matrix associated with
(\tilde{\bm{a}}(s);\tilde{\bm{b}}(s))
(as in

) by
\tilde{\bm{L}}(s)=[\tilde{L}_{ij}(s)]\in\SymMat_{\tilde{N}\times\tilde{N}}
, and set
\eig\tilde{\bm{L}}(s)=(\tilde{\lambda}_{1},\tilde{\lambda}_{2},\ldots,\tilde{\lambda}_{\tilde{N}})
. Setting
\tilde{\zeta}=e^{-100(\log\tilde{N})^{3/2}}
, for each
s\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}

denote a
\zeta
-localization center bijection for
\tilde{\bm{L}}(s)
. Further let
(\tilde{\bm{p}}(s);\tilde{\bm{q}}(s))\in\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{N}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{N}}
denote the Toda state space variables associated with
(\tilde{\bm{a}}(s);\tilde{\bm{b}}(s))
, as in
Section

1.2
, where we have indexed the
\tilde{N}
-tuples

and
\tilde{\bm{q}}(s)=(\tilde{q}_{\tilde{N}_{1}}(s),\tilde{q}_{\tilde{N}_{1}+1}(s),\ldots,\tilde{q}_{\tilde{N}_{2}}(s))
. For each

and

, denote

.

We next restrict to seven events.
12
12

12

.
Recalling

To define the sixth event, set

, and observe by

\displaystyle\displaystyle\max_{i,j\in\llbracket N_{1}+K,N_{2}-K\rrbracket}|M_{ij}-L_{ij}(t)|\leq e^{-(\log N)^{3}};\quad\displaystyle\max_{i,j\in\llbracket N_{1}+K,N_{2}-K\rrbracket}|\tilde{M}_{ij}-\tilde{L}_{ij}(t)|\leq e^{-(\log N)^{3}}.

We may further assume on

(with the
A
there equal to

here, using our restriction to

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\displaystyle\max_{i\in\llbracket N_{1}+K,N_{2}-K\rrbracket}\big(|a_{i}(t)-\tilde{a}_{i}(t)|+|b_{i}(t)-\tilde{b}_{i}(t)|\big)\leq e^{-(\log N)^{3}}.

We may therefore (by (

) and (

)) further restrict to the event

on which

3.19
and

both hold, with the
(\delta;\mathcal{D})
there equal to

here, and the
(\bm{L},\tilde{\bm{L}})
equal to any of

,
(\bm{M},\bm{L}(t))
,
(\tilde{\bm{M}},\tilde{\bm{L}}(t))
, and

here (viewing

as a

if

, and similarly for

).

Now, by

8.4
(and our restriction to

), we have for any index

satisfying

that

(8.26)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|\tilde{Q}_{j}(t)-\tilde{Q}_{j}(0)-tv_{\eff}(\tilde{\lambda}_{j})\big|\leq T^{1/2}(\log\tilde{N})^{33}\leq T^{1/2}\cdot(30\log N)^{33}.

We must therefore approximate
\tilde{Q}_{j}(s)
by
Q_{j}(s)
and

, which we will do using

3.16
,

3.19
, and

(with our restriction to

).

To do so, fix

with

\displaystyle N_{1}+3T(\log\tilde{N})^{4}\leq\varphi_{0}(j)\leq N_{2}-3T(\log\tilde{N})^{4},

which satisfies (
1.16
). By

3.21
(with our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{4}
), we have

(8.28)

\displaystyle N_{1}+2T(\log\tilde{N})^{4}\leq\varphi_{t}(j)\leq N_{2}-2T(\log\tilde{N})^{4}.

By

3.20
(with our restriction to
\mathsf{E}_{7}
), there exists a constant
c_{2}>0
and an eigenvalue
\mu\in\eig\bm{M}
satisfying the following properties. We have that
|\mu-\lambda_{j}|\leq c_{2}^{-1}e^{-c_{2}(\log N)^{3}}
and
\varphi_{t}(j)
is a
N^{-1}\zeta
-localization center of
\mu
with repect to
\bm{M}
. Again by

3.20
, there exists an eigenvalue
\tilde{\mu}\in\eig\tilde{\bm{M}}
such that
